A long-sleeved V-neck cotton T-shirt, a real plain T-shirt and not a real garment, covered edge to edge in a bold flat-colour illustration with thick black outlines, designed to make the shirt look like the outfit of a Maasai warrior. The print covers every part of the shirt from the ribbed collar band to the hem and out to both sleeve hems, and wraps around the side seams to the back. The shirt's neckline is the outfit's collar opening, the shirt's sleeves carry the outfit's sleeves, and the shirt's hem is the outfit's lower edge, showing a red shuka draped over one shoulder with bead collars. The print wraps all the way round, the back of the shirt shows the rear of the outfit from the back collar to the hem and along both sleeves. The outfit's fabric runs seamlessly over the shoulders and down the sleeves, every part of the shirt in the outfit's own colours and textures. It is an ordinary T-shirt in shape, with a straight open hem and sleeves cut to match the outfit's sleeves. The shirt is an ordinary T-shirt ending in a straight bottom hem, and the garment printed on it runs straight down the whole shirt to that hem. The outfit's collar meets the real collar band, its shoulders sit on the real shoulder seams and its sleeves fill the real sleeves, all in one piece of printed cloth. The outfit is laid out on the shirt's own rectangle shape, fully covering it from side seam to side seam. A long-sleeved shirt is used so the outfit's long sleeves fit exactly, printed full length to the shirt's wrist hems. Everything is flat ink on opaque cotton, lace, tulle, fur, feathers, sequins, armour and metal appear as printed patterns. Only the outfit's fabric, trims and fastenings are pictured, spread across the entire shirt as a continuous pattern. It also shows a printed collar continuing the outfit up to the neckline and printed gloves emerging at the sleeve hems. The illusion is purely flat and two-dimensional, with no real fabric, buttons or pockets beyond the print. It carries enough of the costume to be unmistakable, the headwear's emblem or crest worked into the chest, the lower garment's colours and pattern as a band across the hem and the outfit's patches, insignia and emblems, all in the outfit's own colours.

It also shows a printed collar around the real neckline and printed cuffs where the sleeves end. It is the kind of novelty tee that tricks the eye from across a room. Everything is flat ink on opaque cotton, lace, tulle, fur, feathers, sequins, armour and metal appear as printed patterns. Only the outfit's fabric, trims and fastenings are pictured, spread across the entire shirt as a continuous pattern. It is a trompe-l'oeil T-shirt, a plain cotton shirt pretending to be the outfit. The shirt is a standard T-shirt shape with a straight hem across the bottom. The shirt is an ordinary T-shirt ending in a straight bottom hem, and the garment printed on it runs straight down the whole shirt to that hem. The outfit's collar meets the real collar band, its shoulders sit on the real shoulder seams and its sleeves fill the real sleeves, all in one piece of printed cloth. The outfit is stretched to the shirt's own straight-sided shape and fills it completely, so it suits any body size. The shirt is a long-sleeved T-shirt matching the outfit's long sleeves, printed with the sleeves and cuffs all the way down to the wrist hems. The shirt is solid opaque cotton and every texture and sheen is a printed picture. The print is the garment itself, flat and unfolded over the shirt, in its own cloth and colours. It also shows printed cuffs where the sleeves end. It is the kind of novelty tee that tricks the eye from across a room. The outfit's own cloth covers the whole shirt as one continuous print, so shoulders, sleeves and body are all in the outfit's colours. The shirt keeps the outline of an ordinary T-shirt, round neckline, straight side seams, sleeves, and a straight open hem. The printed top garment continues down the shirt to its straight hem, with at most a belt or sash at the waist. The print is cut exactly to the shirt, neck, shoulders, armholes, sleeves and hem are all covered in the outfit's own fabric. The outfit is stretched to the shirt's own straight-sided shape and fills it completely, so it suits any body size. The shirt is a long-sleeved T-shirt matching the outfit's long sleeves, printed with the sleeves and cuffs all the way down to the wrist hems. The shirt is solid opaque cotton and every texture and sheen is a printed picture. The picture is only the garment, its cloth, seams, buttons and trims laid flat across the whole shirt like a sewing pattern. It also shows printed cuffs where the sleeves end. Tiny printed shadows and fold lines suggest the garment without any real depth. The tee is clearly a costume tee, not a plain shirt, it shows its pockets, flaps and trims, the headwear's emblem or crest worked into the chest and the lower garment's colours and pattern as a band across the hem, so anyone can tell at a glance what outfit it stands for.

It is a flat print, not a framed picture, not a small centred graphic, not a standing figure and not a drawing of a garment on a hanger. The outfit's front panels run straight down the shirt's front with its shoulders sitting on the shirt's shoulder seams, and its sleeves are printed along the shirt's sleeves to cuffs at the sleeve hems, showing a wool sack coat. Turn the shirt around and the back carries the matching back of the outfit, printed edge to edge with nothing left plain. The outfit's fabric runs seamlessly over the shoulders and down the sleeves, every part of the shirt in the outfit's own colours and textures. It is an ordinary T-shirt in shape, with a straight open hem and sleeves cut to match the outfit's sleeves. Only the upper garment is printed, running all the way down to the shirt's straight hem. The print is cut exactly to the shirt, neck, shoulders, armholes, sleeves and hem are all covered in the outfit's own fabric. The outfit is stretched to the shirt's own straight-sided shape and fills it completely, so it suits any body size. The shirt is a long-sleeved T-shirt matching the outfit's long sleeves, printed with the sleeves and cuffs all the way down to the wrist hems. Everything is flat ink on opaque cotton, lace, tulle, fur, feathers, sequins, armour and metal appear as printed patterns. Only the outfit's fabric, trims and fastenings are pictured, spread across the entire shirt as a continuous pattern. It also shows a printed collar around the real neckline and printed cuffs where the sleeves end. It is the kind of novelty tee that tricks the eye from across a room. To read as a costume rather than a plain top, it adds the belt, straps and buckles of the outfit, the outfit's patches, insignia and emblems and the footwear's trim colours as stripes on the cuffs and hem, bold and large enough to identify the outfit from across a room.
